On 15 May 1940, Himmler issued a memo entitled "Some Thoughts on the Treatment of Alien Population in the East", calling for the expulsion of the entire Jewish population of Europe into Africa and the reduction of the Polish population to a "leaderless class of labourers". Hitler called Himmler's memo "good and correct", and, ignoring Göring and Frank, implemented the Himmler–Greiser policy in Poland.[275]

[](/wiki/File:Hitler,_Speer_y_Breker_en_Par%C3%ADs,_23_de_junio_de_1940.jpg)Hitler in front of the [Eiffel Tower](/wiki/Eiffel_Tower "Eiffel Tower") in [Paris](/wiki/Paris "Paris") with architect [Albert Speer](/wiki/Albert_Speer "Albert Speer") (left) and sculptor [Arno Breker](/wiki/Arno_Breker "Arno Breker") (right), 23 June 1940

On 9 April, German forces [invaded Denmark and Norway](/wiki/Operation_Weser%C3%BCbung "Operation Weserübung"). On the same day, Hitler proclaimed the birth of the [Greater Germanic Reich](/wiki/Greater_Germanic_Reich "Greater Germanic Reich"), his vision of a united empire of Germanic nations of Europe in which the Dutch, Flemish, and Scandinavians were joined into a "racially pure" polity under German leadership.[276] In May 1940, Germany [attacked France](/wiki/Battle_of_France "Battle of France"), and conquered [Luxembourg](/wiki/German_occupation_of_Luxembourg_in_World_War_II "German occupation of Luxembourg in World War II"), the [Netherlands](/wiki/Battle_of_the_Netherlands "Battle of the Netherlands"), and [Belgium](/wiki/Battle_of_Belgium "Battle of Belgium"). These victories prompted Mussolini to have Italy join forces with Hitler on 10 June. France and Germany signed an [armistice](/wiki/Armistice_of_22_June_1940 "Armistice of 22 June 1940") on 22 June.[277] Kershaw notes that Hitler's popularity within Germany—and German support for the war—reached its peak when he returned to Berlin on 6 July from his tour of Paris.[278] Following the unexpected swift victory, Hitler promoted 12 generals to the rank of [field marshal](/wiki/Field_marshal "Field marshal") during the [1940 Field Marshal Ceremony](/wiki/1940_Field_Marshal_Ceremony "1940 Field Marshal Ceremony").[279][280]

Britain, whose troops were forced to evacuate France by sea from [Dunkirk](/wiki/Dunkirk_evacuation "Dunkirk evacuation"),[281] continued to fight alongside other British [dominions](/wiki/Dominion "Dominion") in the [Battle of the Atlantic](/wiki/Battle_of_the_Atlantic "Battle of the Atlantic"). Hitler made peace overtures to the new British prime minister, [Winston Churchill](/wiki/Winston_Churchill "Winston Churchill"), and upon their rejection, he ordered a series of aerial attacks on [Royal Air Force](/wiki/Royal_Air_Force "Royal Air Force") airbases and radar stations in [southeast England](/wiki/South_East_England "South East England"). On 7 September, the systematic nightly bombing of London began. The German Luftwaffe failed to defeat the Royal Air Force in what became known as the [Battle of Britain](/wiki/Battle_of_Britain "Battle of Britain").[282] By the end of September, Hitler realised that air superiority for the invasion of Britain (in [Operation Sea Lion](/wiki/Operation_Sea_Lion "Operation Sea Lion")) could not be achieved, and ordered the operation postponed. The [nightly air raids](/wiki/The_Blitz "The Blitz") on British cities intensified and continued for months, including London, [Plymouth](/wiki/Plymouth "Plymouth"), and [Coventry](/wiki/Coventry "Coventry").[283]

On 27 September 1940, the [Tripartite Pact](/wiki/Tripartite_Pact "Tripartite Pact") was signed in Berlin by [Saburō Kurusu](/wiki/Sabur%C5%8D_Kurusu "Saburō Kurusu") of [Imperial Japan](/wiki/Imperial_Japan "Imperial Japan"), Hitler, and Italian foreign minister Ciano,[284] and later expanded to include Hungary, Romania, and [Bulgaria](/wiki/Bulgaria "Bulgaria"), thus yielding the [Axis powers](/wiki/Axis_powers "Axis powers"). Hitler's attempt to integrate the Soviet Union into the anti-British bloc failed after inconclusive talks between Hitler and [Molotov](/wiki/Vyacheslav_Molotov "Vyacheslav Molotov") in Berlin in November, and he ordered preparations for the invasion of the Soviet Union.[285]

In early 1941, German forces were deployed to North Africa, the [Balkans](/wiki/Balkans "Balkans"), and the Middle East. In February, [German forces arrived in Libya](/wiki/Operation_Sonnenblume "Operation Sonnenblume") to bolster the Italian presence. In April, Hitler launched the [invasion of Yugoslavia](/wiki/Invasion_of_Yugoslavia "Invasion of Yugoslavia"), quickly followed by the [invasion of Greece](/wiki/Battle_of_Greece "Battle of Greece").[286] In May, German forces were sent to support [Iraqi forces fighting against the British](/wiki/Anglo-Iraqi_War "Anglo-Iraqi War") and to [invade Crete](/wiki/Battle_of_Crete "Battle of Crete").[287] On 28 November, Hitler met in Berlin with [Amin al-Husseini](/wiki/Amin_al-Husseini "Amin al-Husseini"), the [Grand Mufti of Jerusalem](/wiki/Grand_Mufti_of_Jerusalem "Grand Mufti of Jerusalem").[288] Hitler framed opposition to a [Jewish homeland](/wiki/Homeland_for_the_Jewish_people "Homeland for the Jewish people") as part of Germany's broader "struggle against the Jews."[289]

### Path to defeat

[](/wiki/File:Bundesarchiv_Bild_183-1987-0703-507,_Berlin,_Reichstagssitzung,_Rede_Adolf_Hitler.jpg)Hitler [announcing the declaration of war against the United States](/wiki/German_declaration_of_war_against_the_United_States "German declaration of war against the United States") to the _Reichstag_ on 11 December 1941 [](/wiki/File:Hitler_Mannerheim_2.jpg)Adolf Hitler and [Carl Gustaf Emil Mannerheim](/wiki/Carl_Gustaf_Emil_Mannerheim "Carl Gustaf Emil Mannerheim") in Finland in June 1942

On 22 June 1941, contravening the [Molotov–Ribbentrop Pact](/wiki/Molotov%E2%80%93Ribbentrop_Pact "Molotov–Ribbentrop Pact") of 1939, over three million Axis troops attacked the Soviet Union.[290] This offensive (codenamed [Operation Barbarossa](/wiki/Operation_Barbarossa "Operation Barbarossa")) was intended to destroy the Soviet Union and seize its natural resources for subsequent aggression against the Western powers.[291][292] The action was also part of the overall plan to obtain more living space for German people; and Hitler thought a successful invasion would force Britain to negotiate a surrender.[293] The invasion conquered a huge area, including the [Baltic](/wiki/Baltic_region "Baltic region") republics, [Belarus](/wiki/Byelorussian_Soviet_Socialist_Republic "Byelorussian Soviet Socialist Republic"), and West [Ukraine](/wiki/Ukrainian_Soviet_Socialist_Republic "Ukrainian Soviet Socialist Republic"). By early August, Axis troops had advanced 500 km (310 miles) and won the [Battle of Smolensk](/wiki/Battle_of_Smolensk_\(1941\) "Battle of Smolensk \(1941\)"). Hitler ordered [Army Group Centre](/wiki/Army_Group_Centre "Army Group Centre") to temporarily halt its advance to Moscow and divert its Panzer groups to aid in the [encirclement of Leningrad](/wiki/Siege_of_Leningrad "Siege of Leningrad") and [Kiev](/wiki/Battle_of_Kiev_\(1941\) "Battle of Kiev \(1941\)").[294] His generals disagreed with this change, having advanced within 400 km (250 miles) of Moscow, and his decision caused a crisis among the military leadership.[295][296] The pause provided the Red Army with an opportunity to mobilise fresh reserves; the historian Russel Stolfi considers it to be one of the major factors that caused the failure of the Moscow offensive, which was resumed in October 1941 and [ended disastrously in December](/wiki/Battle_of_Moscow "Battle of Moscow").[294] During this crisis, Hitler appointed himself as head of the _[Oberkommando des Heeres](/wiki/Oberkommando_des_Heeres "Oberkommando des Heeres")_.[297]

On 7 December 1941, Japan [attacked the American fleet](/wiki/Attack_on_Pearl_Harbor "Attack on Pearl Harbor") based at [Pearl Harbor](/wiki/Pearl_Harbor "Pearl Harbor"), Hawaii. Four days later, Hitler [declared war against the United States](/wiki/German_declaration_of_war_against_the_United_States "German declaration of war against the United States").[298] On 18 December 1941, Himmler asked Hitler, "What to do with the Jews of Russia?", to which Hitler replied, _"als Partisanen auszurotten"_ ("exterminate them as partisans").[299] The Israeli historian [Yehuda Bauer](/wiki/Yehuda_Bauer "Yehuda Bauer") has commented that the remark is probably as close as historians will ever get to a definitive order from Hitler for the genocide carried out during the Holocaust.[299]

In late 1942, German forces were defeated in the [Second Battle of El Alamein](/wiki/Second_Battle_of_El_Alamein "Second Battle of El Alamein"),[300] thwarting Hitler's plans to seize the [Suez Canal](/wiki/Suez_Canal "Suez Canal") and the Middle East. Overconfident in his own military expertise following the earlier victories in 1940, Hitler became distrustful of his Army High Command and began to interfere in military and tactical planning, with damaging consequences.[301] In December 1942 and January 1943, Hitler's repeated refusal to allow their withdrawal at the [Battle of Stalingrad](/wiki/Battle_of_Stalingrad "Battle of Stalingrad") led to the near-destruction of the [6th Army](/wiki/6th_Army_\(Wehrmacht\) "6th Army \(Wehrmacht\)"). Over 200,000 Axis soldiers were killed, and 235,000 were taken prisoner.[302] Thereafter came a decisive strategic defeat at the [Battle of Kursk](/wiki/Battle_of_Kursk "Battle of Kursk").[303] Hitler's military judgment became increasingly erratic, and Germany's military and economic position deteriorated, as did Hitler's health.[304]

[](/wiki/File:Bundesarchiv_Bild_146-1972-025-12,_Zerst%C3%B6rte_Lagerbaracke_nach_dem_20._Juli_1944.jpg)The destroyed map room at the [Wolf's Lair](/wiki/Wolf%27s_Lair "Wolf's Lair"), Hitler's eastern command post, after the [20 July plot](/wiki/20_July_plot "20 July plot")

Following the [Allied invasion of Sicily](/wiki/Allied_invasion_of_Sicily "Allied invasion of Sicily") in 1943, [Mussolini was removed from power](/wiki/Fall_of_the_Fascist_regime_in_Italy "Fall of the Fascist regime in Italy") by King [Victor Emmanuel III](/wiki/Victor_Emmanuel_III "Victor Emmanuel III") after a vote of no confidence of the [Grand Council of Fascism](/wiki/Grand_Council_of_Fascism "Grand Council of Fascism"). Marshal [Pietro Badoglio](/wiki/Pietro_Badoglio "Pietro Badoglio"), placed in charge of the government, soon [surrendered to the Allies](/wiki/Armistice_of_Cassibile "Armistice of Cassibile").[305] Throughout 1943 and 1944, the Soviet Union steadily forced Hitler's armies into retreat along the [Eastern Front](/wiki/Eastern_Front_\(World_War_II\) "Eastern Front \(World War II\)"). On 6 June 1944, the Western Allied armies landed in northern France in one of the largest [amphibious](/wiki/Amphibious_warfare "Amphibious warfare") operations in history, [Operation Overlord](/wiki/Operation_Overlord "Operation Overlord").[306] Many German officers concluded that defeat was inevitable and that continuing under Hitler's leadership would result in the [complete destruction of the country](/wiki/Total_war "Total war").[307]

Between 1939 and 1945, there were numerous plans to [assassinate Hitler](/wiki/Assassination_attempts_on_Adolf_Hitler "Assassination attempts on Adolf Hitler"), some of which proceeded to significant degrees.[308] The most well-known and significant, the [20 July plot](/wiki/20_July_plot "20 July plot") of 1944, came from within Germany and was at least partly driven by the increasing prospect of a German defeat in the war.[309] Part of [Operation Valkyrie](/wiki/Operation_Valkyrie "Operation Valkyrie"), the plot involved [Claus von Stauffenberg](/wiki/Claus_von_Stauffenberg "Claus von Stauffenberg") planting a bomb in one of [Hitler's headquarters](/wiki/F%C3%BChrer_Headquarters "Führer Headquarters"), the [Wolf's Lair](/wiki/Wolf%27s_Lair "Wolf's Lair") at [Rastenburg](/wiki/Rastenburg "Rastenburg"). Hitler narrowly survived because the staff officer [Heinz Brandt](/wiki/Heinz_Brandt "Heinz Brandt") moved the briefcase containing the bomb behind a leg of the heavy conference table, which deflected much of the blast. Later, Hitler ordered reprisals, resulting in the execution of more than 4,900 people.[310] Hitler was put on the [United Nations War Crimes Commission](/wiki/United_Nations_War_Crimes_Commission "United Nations War Crimes Commission")'s first list of [war criminals](/wiki/War_criminal "War criminal") in December 1944, after determining that Hitler could be held criminally responsible for the acts of the Nazis in occupied countries. By March 1945, at least seven indictments had been filed against him.[311]

### Defeat and death

Main article: [Death of Adolf Hitler](/wiki/Death_of_Adolf_Hitler "Death of Adolf Hitler")

[](/wiki/File:Adolf_Hitler,_20._april_1945.jpg_\(2\).jpg)Hitler in his last filmed appearance, honouring Hitler Youth members of the _[Volkssturm](/wiki/Volkssturm "Volkssturm")_ in the Reich Chancellery garden [](/wiki/File:Stars_%26_Stripes_%26_Hitler_Dead2.jpg)Front page of the US Armed Forces newspaper, _[Stars and Stripes](/wiki/Stars_and_Stripes_\(newspaper\) "Stars and Stripes \(newspaper\)")_ , 2 May 1945, announcing Hitler's death. It erroneously states that Hitler died on 1 May; he died on 30 April.

By late 1944, both the Red Army and the [Western Allies](/wiki/Western_Allies "Western Allies") were advancing into Germany. Recognising the strength and determination of the Red Army, Hitler decided to use his remaining mobile reserves against the American and British armies, which he perceived as far weaker.[312] On 16 December, he launched the [Ardennes Offensive](/wiki/Ardennes_Offensive "Ardennes Offensive") to incite disunity among the Western Allies and perhaps convince them to join his fight against the Soviets.[313] After some temporary successes, the offensive failed.[314] With much of Germany in ruins in January 1945, Hitler spoke on the radio: "However grave as the crisis may be at this moment, it will, despite everything, be mastered by our unalterable will."[315] On 19 March, Hitler commented that the needs of the German population could now be disregarded, because they "had proven to be the weaker, and the future belongs solely to the stronger eastern nation. In any case only those who are inferior will remain after this struggle, for the good have already been killed".[316] The same day, Hitler [ordered the destruction of all German industrial infrastructure](/wiki/Nero_Decree "Nero Decree") before it could fall into Allied hands.[317] Minister for Armaments [Albert Speer](/wiki/Albert_Speer "Albert Speer") was entrusted with executing this [scorched earth](/wiki/Scorched_earth "Scorched earth") policy, but he secretly disobeyed the order.[317][318] Hitler's hope to negotiate peace with the United States and Britain was encouraged by the death of US President [Franklin D. Roosevelt](/wiki/Franklin_D._Roosevelt "Franklin D. Roosevelt") on 12 April 1945, but contrary to his expectations, this caused no rift among the Allies.[313][319]

On 20 April, his 56th birthday, Hitler made his last trip from the _Führerbunker_ to the surface. In the ruined garden of the Reich Chancellery, he awarded Iron Crosses to boy soldiers of the [Hitler Youth](/wiki/Hitler_Youth "Hitler Youth"), who were now fighting the Red Army at the front near Berlin.[320] By 21 April, [Georgy Zhukov](/wiki/Georgy_Zhukov "Georgy Zhukov")'s [1st Belorussian Front](/wiki/1st_Belorussian_Front "1st Belorussian Front") had broken through the defences of General [Gotthard Heinrici](/wiki/Gotthard_Heinrici "Gotthard Heinrici")'s [Army Group Vistula](/wiki/Army_Group_Vistula "Army Group Vistula") during the [Battle of the Seelow Heights](/wiki/Battle_of_the_Seelow_Heights "Battle of the Seelow Heights") and advanced to the outskirts of Berlin.[321] In denial about the dire situation, Hitler placed his hopes on the undermanned and under-equipped _Armeeabteilung Steiner_ ([Army Detachment Steiner](/wiki/Army_Detachment_Steiner "Army Detachment Steiner")), commanded by [Felix Steiner](/wiki/Felix_Steiner "Felix Steiner"). Hitler ordered Steiner to attack the northern flank of the [salient](/wiki/Salients,_re-entrants_and_pockets "Salients, re-entrants and pockets"), while the German [Ninth Army](/wiki/9th_Army_\(Wehrmacht\) "9th Army \(Wehrmacht\)") was ordered to attack northward in a [pincer attack](/wiki/Pincer_attack "Pincer attack").[322]

During a military conference on 22 April, Hitler enquired about Steiner's offensive. He was informed that the attack had not been launched and that the Soviets had entered Berlin. Hitler ordered everyone but Wilhelm Keitel, [Alfred Jodl](/wiki/Alfred_Jodl "Alfred Jodl"), [Hans Krebs](/wiki/Hans_Krebs_\(Wehrmacht_general\) "Hans Krebs \(Wehrmacht general\)"), and [Wilhelm Burgdorf](/wiki/Wilhelm_Burgdorf "Wilhelm Burgdorf") to leave the room,[323] then launched into a tirade against the perceived treachery and incompetence of his generals, culminating in his declaration—for the first time—that "everything is lost".[324] He announced that he would stay in Berlin until the end and then shoot himself.[325]

By 23 April, the Red Army had surrounded Berlin,[326] and Goebbels made a proclamation urging its citizens to defend the city.[323] That same day, Göring sent a telegram from [Berchtesgaden](/wiki/Berchtesgaden "Berchtesgaden"), arguing that as Hitler was isolated in Berlin, Göring should assume leadership of Germany. Göring set a deadline, after which he would consider Hitler incapacitated.[327] Hitler responded by having Göring arrested, and in his [last will and testament](/wiki/Last_will_and_testament_of_Adolf_Hitler "Last will and testament of Adolf Hitler") of 29 April, he removed Göring from all government positions.[328][329] On 28 April, Hitler discovered that Himmler, who had left Berlin on 20 April, was attempting to negotiate a surrender to the Western Allies.[330][331] He considered this treason and ordered Himmler's arrest. He also ordered the execution of [Hermann Fegelein](/wiki/Hermann_Fegelein "Hermann Fegelein"), Himmler's SS representative at Hitler's headquarters in Berlin, for desertion.[332]

After midnight on the night of 28–29 April, Hitler married Eva Braun in a small civil ceremony in the _Führerbunker_.[333][f] Later that afternoon, Hitler was informed that [Mussolini had been executed](/wiki/Death_of_Benito_Mussolini "Death of Benito Mussolini") by the [Italian resistance movement](/wiki/Italian_resistance_movement "Italian resistance movement") on the previous day; this is believed to have increased his determination to avoid capture.[334] On 30 April, Soviet troops were within five hundred metres of the Reich Chancellery when Hitler shot himself in the head and Braun bit into a [cyanide](/wiki/Cyanide "Cyanide") capsule.[335][336] In accordance with Hitler's wishes, their corpses were carried outside to the garden behind the Reich Chancellery, where they were placed in a bomb crater, doused with petrol, and set on fire as the Red Army shelling continued.[337][338][339] Grand Admiral [Karl Dönitz](/wiki/Karl_D%C3%B6nitz "Karl Dönitz") and Goebbels assumed Hitler's roles as head of state and chancellor respectively.[340] On the evening of 1 May, Goebbels and his wife, [Magda](/wiki/Magda_Goebbels "Magda Goebbels"), committed suicide in the Reich Chancellery garden, after having poisoned their six children with cyanide.[341]

[Berlin surrendered](/wiki/Battle_of_Berlin "Battle of Berlin") on 2 May. The remains of the Goebbels family, General [Hans Krebs](/wiki/Hans_Krebs_\(Wehrmacht_general\) "Hans Krebs \(Wehrmacht general\)") (who had committed suicide that day), and Hitler's dog [Blondi](/wiki/Blondi "Blondi") were repeatedly buried and exhumed by the Soviets.[342] Hitler's and Braun's remains were alleged to have been moved as well, but this is most likely Soviet [disinformation](/wiki/Disinformation "Disinformation"). There is no evidence that any identifiable remains of Hitler or Braun—with the exception of dental bridges—were ever found by them.[343][344][345] While news of Hitler's death spread quickly, a [death certificate](/wiki/Death_certificate "Death certificate") was not issued until 1956, after a lengthy investigation to collect testimony from 42 witnesses. Hitler's death was entered as an [assumption of death](/wiki/Presumption_of_death "Presumption of death") based on this testimony.[346]

## The Holocaust

Main articles: [The Holocaust](/wiki/The_Holocaust "The Holocaust") and [Final Solution](/wiki/Final_Solution "Final Solution")

> [If the international Jewish financiers in and outside Europe should succeed in plunging the nations once more into a world war, then the result will not be the Bolshevisation of the earth, and thus the victory of Jewry, but the annihilation of the Jewish race in Europe!](/wiki/Hitler%27s_prophecy "Hitler's prophecy")[347]

— Adolf Hitler, [30 January 1939 Reichstag speech](/wiki/30_January_1939_Reichstag_speech "30 January 1939 Reichstag speech")

[](/wiki/File:Mass_Grave_3_at_Bergen-Belsen_concentration_camp.jpg)A mass grave at [Bergen-Belsen](/wiki/Bergen-Belsen "Bergen-Belsen") after the camp's liberation, April 1945

The Holocaust and Germany's war in the East were based on Hitler's long-standing view that the Jews were the enemy of the German people, and that _[Lebensraum](/wiki/Lebensraum "Lebensraum")_ was needed for Germany's expansion. He focused on Eastern Europe for this expansion, aiming to defeat Poland and the Soviet Union and then removing or killing the Jews and [Slavs](/wiki/Slavs "Slavs").[348] The _[Generalplan Ost](/wiki/Generalplan_Ost "Generalplan Ost")_ (General Plan East) called for deporting the population of occupied Eastern Europe and the Soviet Union to West Siberia, for use as slave labour or to be murdered;[349] the conquered territories were to be colonised by German or "Germanised" settlers.[350] The goal was to implement this plan after the conquest of the Soviet Union, but when this failed, Hitler moved the plans forward.[349][351] By January 1942, he had decided that the Jews, Slavs, and other deportees considered undesirable should be killed.[352][g]

[](/wiki/File:Erlass_von_Hitler_-_N%C3%BCrnberger_Dokument_PS-630_-_datiert_1._September_1939.jpg)Hitler's order for _[Aktion T4](/wiki/Aktion_T4 "Aktion T4")_ , dated 1 September 1939

The genocide was organised and executed by [Heinrich Himmler](/wiki/Heinrich_Himmler "Heinrich Himmler") and [Reinhard Heydrich](/wiki/Reinhard_Heydrich "Reinhard Heydrich"). The records of the [Wannsee Conference](/wiki/Wannsee_Conference "Wannsee Conference"), held on 20 January 1942 and led by Heydrich, with 15 senior Nazi officials participating, provide the clearest evidence of systematic planning for the Holocaust. On 22 February, Hitler was recorded saying, "we shall regain our health only by eliminating the Jews".[353] Similarly, at a meeting in July 1941 with leading functionaries of the Eastern territories, Hitler said that the easiest way to quickly [pacify](/wiki/Pacification_\(military_action\) "Pacification \(military action\)") the areas would be best achieved by "shooting everyone who even looks odd".[354] Although no direct order from Hitler authorising the mass killings has surfaced,[355] his public speeches, orders to his generals,[_[contradictory](/wiki/Category:All_self-contradictory_articles "Category:All self-contradictory articles")_] and the diaries of Nazi officials demonstrate that he conceived and authorised the extermination of European Jewry.[356][357] During the war, Hitler repeatedly stated his [prophecy of 1939](/wiki/Hitler%27s_prophecy "Hitler's prophecy") was being fulfilled, namely, that a world war would bring about the annihilation of the Jewish race.[358] Hitler approved the _[Einsatzgruppen](/wiki/Einsatzgruppen "Einsatzgruppen")_ —killing squads that followed the German army through Poland, the Baltic, and the Soviet Union[359]—and was well informed about their activities.[356][360] By summer 1942, [Auschwitz concentration camp](/wiki/Auschwitz_concentration_camp "Auschwitz concentration camp") was expanded to accommodate large numbers of deportees for murder or [enslavement](/wiki/Forced_labour_under_German_rule_during_World_War_II "Forced labour under German rule during World War II").[361] Scores of other concentration camps and satellite camps were set up throughout Europe, with [several camps devoted exclusively to extermination](/wiki/Extermination_camp "Extermination camp").[362]

Between 1939 and 1945, the _[Schutzstaffel](/wiki/Schutzstaffel "Schutzstaffel")_ (SS), assisted by [collaborationist](/wiki/Collaboration_with_Nazi_Germany_and_Fascist_Italy "Collaboration with Nazi Germany and Fascist Italy") governments and recruits from occupied countries, were responsible for the deaths of at least 11 million non-combatants,[363][349] including the murders of about six million Jews (representing two-thirds of the Jewish population of Europe),[364][h] and between 200,000 and 1,500,000 [Romani people](/wiki/Porajmos "Porajmos").[366][364] The victims were killed in concentration and extermination camps and in [ghettos](/wiki/Ghettos_in_Nazi-occupied_Europe "Ghettos in Nazi-occupied Europe"), and through mass shootings.[367][368] Many victims of the Holocaust were murdered in [gas chambers](/wiki/Gas_chamber "Gas chamber") or shot, while others died of starvation or disease or [while working as slave labourers](/wiki/Extermination_through_labour "Extermination through labour").[367][368] In addition to eliminating Jews, the Nazis planned to reduce the population of the conquered territories by 30 million people through starvation in an action called the [Hunger Plan](/wiki/Hunger_Plan "Hunger Plan"). Food supplies would be diverted to the German army and German civilians. Cities would be razed, and the land allowed to return to forest or resettled by German colonists.[369] Together, the Hunger Plan and _Generalplan Ost_ would have led to the starvation of 80 million people in the Soviet Union.[370] These partially fulfilled plans resulted in additional deaths, bringing the total number of civilians and prisoners of war who died in the [democide](/wiki/Democide "Democide") to an estimated 19.3 million people.[371]

Hitler's policies resulted in the killing of nearly two million non-Jewish [Polish civilians](/wiki/Nazi_crimes_against_the_Polish_nation "Nazi crimes against the Polish nation"),[372] over three million [Soviet prisoners of war](/wiki/German_mistreatment_of_Soviet_prisoners_of_war "German mistreatment of Soviet prisoners of war"),[373] communists and other political opponents, [homosexuals](/wiki/Persecution_of_homosexuals_in_Nazi_Germany "Persecution of homosexuals in Nazi Germany"), the physically and mentally disabled,[374][375] [Jehovah's Witnesses](/wiki/Persecution_of_Jehovah%27s_Witnesses_in_Nazi_Germany "Persecution of Jehovah's Witnesses in Nazi Germany"), [Adventists](/wiki/Adventists "Adventists"), and trade unionists. Hitler never spoke publicly about the killings and seems to have never visited the concentration camps.[376] The Nazis embraced the concept of [racial hygiene](/wiki/Racial_hygiene "Racial hygiene"). On 15 September 1935, Hitler presented two laws—known as the [Nuremberg Laws](/wiki/Nuremberg_Laws "Nuremberg Laws")—to the Reichstag. The laws banned sexual relations and marriages between Aryans and Jews and were later extended to include "Gypsies, Negroes or their bastard offspring".[377] The laws stripped all non-Aryans of their German citizenship and forbade the employment of non-Jewish women under the age of 45 in Jewish households.[378] Hitler's early [eugenic](/wiki/Nazi_eugenics "Nazi eugenics") policies targeted children with physical and developmental disabilities in a programme dubbed [Action Brandt](/wiki/Child_euthanasia_in_Nazi_Germany "Child euthanasia in Nazi Germany"), and he later authorised a [euthanasia](/wiki/Euthanasia "Euthanasia") programme for adults with serious mental and physical disabilities, now referred to as _[Aktion T4](/wiki/Aktion_T4 "Aktion T4")_.[379]

## Leadership style

[](/wiki/File:Bundesarchiv_Bild_183-B24543,_Hauptquartier_Heeresgruppe_S%C3%BCd,_Lagebesprechung.jpg)Hitler during a meeting at the headquarters of [Army Group South](/wiki/Army_Group_South "Army Group South") in June 1942

Hitler ruled the Nazi Party [autocratically](/wiki/Autocratic "Autocratic") by asserting the _[Führerprinzip](/wiki/F%C3%BChrerprinzip "Führerprinzip")_ (leader principle). The principle relied on absolute obedience of all subordinates to their superiors; thus, he viewed the government structure as a pyramid, with himself—the [infallible leader](/wiki/Leaderism "Leaderism")—at the apex. Rank in the party was not determined by elections—positions were filled through appointment by those of higher rank, who demanded unquestioning obedience to the will of the leader.[380] Hitler's leadership style was to give contradictory orders to his subordinates and to place them into positions where their duties and responsibilities overlapped with those of others, to have "the stronger one [do] the job".[381] In this way, Hitler fostered distrust, competition, and infighting among his subordinates to consolidate and maximise his own power. [His cabinet](/wiki/Hitler_Cabinet "Hitler Cabinet") never met after 1938, and he discouraged his ministers from meeting independently.[382][383] Hitler typically did not give written orders; instead, he communicated verbally, or had them conveyed through his close associate [Martin Bormann](/wiki/Martin_Bormann "Martin Bormann").[384] He entrusted Bormann with his paperwork, appointments, and personal finances; Bormann used his position to control the flow of information and access to Hitler.[385]

Hitler dominated his country's war effort during World War II to a greater extent than any other national leader. He strengthened his control of the armed forces in 1938, and subsequently made all major decisions regarding Germany's military strategy. His decision to mount a risky series of offensives against Norway, France, and the Low Countries in 1940, against the advice of the military, proved successful, though the diplomatic and military strategies he employed in attempts to force the United Kingdom out of the war ended in failure.[386] Hitler deepened his involvement in the war effort by appointing himself commander-in-chief of the Army in December 1941; from this point forward, he personally directed the war against the Soviet Union, while his military commanders facing the Western Allies retained a degree of autonomy.[387] Hitler's leadership became increasingly disconnected from reality as the war turned against Germany, with the military's defensive strategies often hindered by his slow decision-making and frequent directives to hold untenable positions. Nevertheless, he continued to believe that only his leadership could deliver victory.[386] In the final months of the war, Hitler refused to consider peace negotiations, regarding the destruction of Germany as preferable to surrender.[388] The military did not challenge Hitler's dominance of the war effort, and senior officers generally supported and enacted his decisions.[389]

## Personal life

### Family

Main article: [Hitler family](/wiki/Hitler_family "Hitler family")

See also: [Sexuality of Adolf Hitler](/wiki/Sexuality_of_Adolf_Hitler "Sexuality of Adolf Hitler")

[](/wiki/File:Bundesarchiv_B_145_Bild-F051673-0059,_Adolf_Hitler_und_Eva_Braun_auf_dem_Berghof.jpg)Hitler and Braun in 1942

Hitler created a public image as a celibate man without a domestic life, dedicated entirely to his political mission and the nation.[154][390] He met his lover, Eva Braun, in 1929,[391] and married her on 29 April 1945, one day before they both committed suicide.[392] In September 1931, his half-niece, [Geli Raubal](/wiki/Geli_Raubal "Geli Raubal"), committed suicide with Hitler's gun in his Munich apartment. It was rumoured among contemporaries that Geli was in a romantic relationship with him, and her death was a source of deep, lasting pain.[393] [Paula Hitler](/wiki/Paula_Hitler "Paula Hitler"), the younger sister of Hitler and the last living member of his immediate family, died in June 1960.[18]

### Views on religion

Main article: [Religious views of Adolf Hitler](/wiki/Religious_views_of_Adolf_Hitler "Religious views of Adolf Hitler")

Hitler was born to a practising [Catholic](/wiki/Catholic "Catholic") mother and an [anti-clerical](/wiki/Anti-clerical "Anti-clerical") father; after leaving home, Hitler never again attended [Mass](/wiki/Mass_in_the_Catholic_Church "Mass in the Catholic Church") or received the [sacraments](/wiki/Sacraments_of_the_Catholic_Church "Sacraments of the Catholic Church").[394][395][396] Albert Speer states that Hitler railed against the church to his political associates, and though he never officially left the church, he had no attachment to it.[397] He adds that Hitler felt that in the absence of organised religion, people would turn to mysticism, which he considered regressive.[397] According to Speer, Hitler believed that [Japanese religious beliefs](/wiki/Religion_in_Japan "Religion in Japan") or [Islam](/wiki/Islam "Islam") would have been a more suitable religion for Germans than Christianity, with its "meekness and flabbiness".[398] The historian [John S. Conway](/wiki/John_S._Conway_\(historian\) "John S. Conway \(historian\)") states that Hitler was fundamentally opposed to the Christian churches.[399] According to Bullock, Hitler did not believe in God, was anticlerical, and held Christian ethics in contempt because they contravened his preferred view of "[survival of the fittest](/wiki/Survival_of_the_fittest "Survival of the fittest")".[400] He favoured aspects of [Protestantism](/wiki/Protestantism "Protestantism") that suited his own views, and adopted some elements of the Catholic Church's hierarchical organisation, [liturgy](/wiki/Liturgy "Liturgy"), and phraseology.[401] In a 1932 speech, Hitler stated that he was not a Catholic, and declared himself a [German Christian](/wiki/German_Christians_\(movement\) "German Christians \(movement\)").[402] In a conversation with Albert Speer, Hitler said, "Through me the [Evangelical Church](/wiki/Evangelicalism "Evangelicalism") could become the established church, as in England."[403]

[](/wiki/File:Hitler_with_Catholic_dignitaries.jpg)Hitler shaking hands with Bishop [Ludwig Müller](/wiki/Ludwig_M%C3%BCller "Ludwig Müller") in 1934

Hitler viewed the church as an important politically conservative influence on society,[404] and he adopted a strategic relationship with it that "suited his immediate political purposes".[399] In public, Hitler often praised Christian heritage and German Christian culture, though professing a belief in an "Aryan Jesus" who fought against the Jews.[405] Privately, he described Christianity as "absurdity"[406] and nonsense founded on lies.[407]

According to a US [Office of Strategic Services](/wiki/Office_of_Strategic_Services "Office of Strategic Services") (OSS) report, "The Nazi Master Plan", Hitler planned to destroy the influence of Christian churches within the Reich.[408][409] His eventual goal was the total elimination of Christianity.[410] This goal informed Hitler's movement early on, but he saw it as inexpedient to publicly express this extreme position.[411] According to Bullock, Hitler wanted to wait until after the war before executing this plan.[412] Speer wrote that Hitler had a negative view of Himmler's and [Alfred Rosenberg](/wiki/Alfred_Rosenberg "Alfred Rosenberg")'s mystical notions and Himmler's attempt to mythologise the SS. Hitler was more pragmatic, and his ambitions centred on more practical concerns.[413][414]

### Health

See also: [Health of Adolf Hitler](/wiki/Health_of_Adolf_Hitler "Health of Adolf Hitler") and [Psychopathography of Adolf Hitler](/wiki/Psychopathography_of_Adolf_Hitler "Psychopathography of Adolf Hitler")

Researchers have variously suggested that Hitler suffered from [irritable bowel syndrome](/wiki/Irritable_bowel_syndrome "Irritable bowel syndrome"), [skin lesions](/wiki/Skin_lesion "Skin lesion"), [irregular heartbeat](/wiki/Irregular_heartbeat "Irregular heartbeat"), [coronary sclerosis](/wiki/Coronary_sclerosis "Coronary sclerosis"),[415] [Parkinson's disease](/wiki/Parkinson%27s_disease "Parkinson's disease"),[304][416] [syphilis](/wiki/Syphilis "Syphilis"),[416] [giant-cell arteritis](/wiki/Giant-cell_arteritis "Giant-cell arteritis"),[417] [tinnitus](/wiki/Tinnitus "Tinnitus"),[418] and [monorchism](/wiki/Monorchism "Monorchism").[419]

In a report prepared for the OSS in 1943, [Walter Charles Langer](/wiki/Walter_Charles_Langer "Walter Charles Langer") of [Harvard University](/wiki/Harvard_University "Harvard University") described Hitler as a "neurotic [psychopath](/wiki/Psychopath "Psychopath")" and speculated that he exhibited masochistic tendencies and deep feelings of self-loathing, which he projected onto others, particularly Jews.[420][421]

In his 1977 book _[The Psychopathic God: Adolf Hitler](/wiki/The_Psychopathic_God:_Adolf_Hitler "The Psychopathic God: Adolf Hitler")_ , the historian [Robert G. L. Waite](/wiki/Robert_G._L._Waite "Robert G. L. Waite") proposes that Hitler suffered from [borderline personality disorder](/wiki/Borderline_personality_disorder "Borderline personality disorder").[422] The historians Henrik Eberle and Hans-Joachim Neumann consider that while he suffered from several illnesses, including Parkinson's disease, Hitler did not experience pathological delusions and was always fully aware of, and therefore responsible for, his decisions.[423][324]

Sometime in the 1930s, [Hitler adopted a mainly vegetarian diet](/wiki/Adolf_Hitler_and_vegetarianism "Adolf Hitler and vegetarianism"),[424][425] avoiding all meat and fish from 1942 onwards. At social events, he sometimes gave graphic accounts of the slaughter of animals in an effort to make his guests shun meat.[426] Bormann had a greenhouse constructed near the [Berghof](/wiki/Berghof_\(residence\) "Berghof \(residence\)") (near [Berchtesgaden](/wiki/Berchtesgaden "Berchtesgaden")) to ensure a steady supply of fresh fruit and vegetables for Hitler.[427] Hitler stopped drinking alcohol around the time he became vegetarian and thereafter only very occasionally drank beer or wine on social occasions.[428][429] He was a non-smoker for most of his adult life, but smoked heavily in his youth (25 to 40 cigarettes a day); he eventually quit, calling the habit "a waste of money".[430] He encouraged his close associates to quit by offering a gold watch to anyone able to break the habit.[431] Hitler began using [amphetamine](/wiki/Amphetamine "Amphetamine") occasionally after 1937 and became addicted to it in late 1942.[432] Speer linked this use of amphetamine to Hitler's increasingly erratic behaviour and inflexible decision-making (for example, rarely allowing military retreats).[433]

Prescribed 90 medications during the war years by his personal physician, [Theodor Morell](/wiki/Theodor_Morell "Theodor Morell"), Hitler took many pills each day for chronic stomach problems and other ailments.[434] He regularly consumed amphetamine, [barbiturates](/wiki/Barbiturate "Barbiturate"), [opiates](/wiki/Opiate "Opiate"), and [cocaine](/wiki/Cocaine "Cocaine"),[435][436] as well as [potassium bromide](/wiki/Potassium_bromide "Potassium bromide") and [atropa belladonna](/wiki/Atropa_belladonna "Atropa belladonna") (the latter in the form of [Doktor Koster's Antigaspills](/wiki/Doktor_Koster%27s_Antigaspills "Doktor Koster's Antigaspills")).[437] He suffered [ruptured eardrums](/wiki/Ruptured_eardrum "Ruptured eardrum") as a result of the [20 July plot](/wiki/20_July_plot "20 July plot") bomb blast in 1944, and 200 wood splinters had to be removed from his legs.[438] Newsreel footage of Hitler shows tremors in his left hand and a shuffling walk, which began before the war and worsened towards the end of his life.[434] [Ernst-Günther Schenck](/wiki/Ernst-G%C3%BCnther_Schenck "Ernst-Günther Schenck") and several other doctors who met Hitler in the last weeks of his life also formed a diagnosis of Parkinson's disease.[439]

## Legacy

Further information: [Historiography of Adolf Hitler](/wiki/Historiography_of_Adolf_Hitler "Historiography of Adolf Hitler"), [Consequences of Nazism](/wiki/Consequences_of_Nazism "Consequences of Nazism"), and [Neo-Nazism](/wiki/Neo-Nazism "Neo-Nazism")

[](/wiki/File:Mahnstein.JPG)Outside of a building in [Braunau am Inn](/wiki/Braunau_am_Inn "Braunau am Inn"), Austria, where Hitler was born, is a [memorial stone](/wiki/Hitler_birthplace_memorial_stone "Hitler birthplace memorial stone") placed as a reminder of World War II. The inscription translates as:[440]

For peace, freedom  
and democracy  
never again fascism  
millions of dead warn [us] 

According to the historian [Joachim Fest](/wiki/Joachim_Fest "Joachim Fest"), Hitler's suicide was likened by numerous contemporaries to a "spell" being broken.[441] Similarly, Speer commented in _[Inside the Third Reich](/wiki/Inside_the_Third_Reich "Inside the Third Reich")_ on his emotions the day after Hitler's suicide: "Only now was the spell broken, the magic extinguished."[442] Public support for Hitler had collapsed by the time of his death, which few Germans mourned; Kershaw argues that most civilians and military personnel were too busy adjusting to the collapse of the country or fleeing from the fighting to take any interest.[443] According to the historian [John Toland](/wiki/John_Toland_\(historian\) "John Toland \(historian\)"), Nazism "burst like a bubble" without its leader.[444]

Kershaw describes Hitler as "the embodiment of modern political evil".[3] "Never in history has such ruination—physical and moral—been associated with the name of one man", he adds.[445] Hitler's political programme brought about a world war, leaving behind a devastated and impoverished Eastern and Central Europe. Germany suffered wholesale destruction, characterised as _[Stunde Null](/wiki/Stunde_Null "Stunde Null")_ (Zero Hour).[446] Hitler's policies inflicted human suffering on an unprecedented scale;[447] according to [R. J. Rummel](/wiki/R._J._Rummel "R. J. Rummel"), the Nazi regime was responsible for the [democidal](/wiki/Democidal "Democidal") killing of an estimated 19.3 million civilians and prisoners of war.[363] In addition, 28.7 million soldiers and civilians died as a result of military action in the [European theatre of World War II](/wiki/European_theatre_of_World_War_II "European theatre of World War II").[363] The number of civilians killed during the Second World War was unprecedented in the history of warfare.[448] Historians, philosophers, and politicians often use the word "evil" to describe the Nazi regime.[449] Many European countries have [criminalised](/wiki/Laws_against_Holocaust_denial "Laws against Holocaust denial") both the promotion of Nazism and [Holocaust denial](/wiki/Holocaust_denial "Holocaust denial").[450]

The historian [Friedrich Meinecke](/wiki/Friedrich_Meinecke "Friedrich Meinecke") described Hitler as "one of the great examples of the singular and incalculable power of personality in historical life".[451] The English historian [Hugh Trevor-Roper](/wiki/Hugh_Trevor-Roper "Hugh Trevor-Roper") saw him as "among the 'terrible simplifiers' of history, the most systematic, the most historical, the most philosophical, and yet the coarsest, cruelest, least magnanimous conqueror the world has ever known".[452] For the historian [John M. Roberts](/wiki/John_Roberts_\(historian\) "John Roberts \(historian\)"), Hitler's defeat marked the end of a phase of European history dominated by Germany.[453] In its place emerged the [Cold War](/wiki/Cold_War "Cold War"), a global confrontation between the [Western Bloc](/wiki/Western_Bloc "Western Bloc"), dominated by the United States and other [NATO](/wiki/NATO "NATO") nations, and the [Eastern Bloc](/wiki/Eastern_Bloc "Eastern Bloc"), dominated by the Soviet Union.[454] The historian [Sebastian Haffner](/wiki/Sebastian_Haffner "Sebastian Haffner") asserted that without Hitler and the displacement of the Jews, the modern nation-state of Israel would not exist. He contends that without Hitler, the [de-colonisation](/wiki/De-colonisation "De-colonisation") of former European spheres of influence would have been postponed.[455] Further, Haffner claimed that other than [Alexander the Great](/wiki/Alexander_the_Great "Alexander the Great"), Hitler had a more significant impact than any other comparable historical figure, in that he too caused a wide range of worldwide changes in a relatively short time span.[456]

### In propaganda

See also: [Adolf Hitler in popular culture](/wiki/Adolf_Hitler_in_popular_culture "Adolf Hitler in popular culture") and [List of speeches given by Adolf Hitler](/wiki/List_of_speeches_given_by_Adolf_Hitler "List of speeches given by Adolf Hitler")

Hitler exploited documentary films and newsreels to inspire a [cult of personality](/wiki/Cult_of_personality "Cult of personality"). He was involved and appeared in a series of propaganda films throughout his political career, many made by [Leni Riefenstahl](/wiki/Leni_Riefenstahl "Leni Riefenstahl"), regarded as a pioneer of modern filmmaking.[457] Hitler's propaganda film appearances include: 

  * _[Der Sieg des Glaubens](/wiki/Der_Sieg_des_Glaubens "Der Sieg des Glaubens")_ (_Victory of Faith_ , 1933)
  * _[Triumph des Willens](/wiki/Triumph_des_Willens "Triumph des Willens")_ (_Triumph of the Will_ , 1935)
  * _[Tag der Freiheit: Unsere Wehrmacht](/wiki/Tag_der_Freiheit:_Unsere_Wehrmacht "Tag der Freiheit: Unsere Wehrmacht")_ (_Day of Freedom: Our Armed Forces_ , 1935)
  * _[Olympia](/wiki/Olympia_\(1938_film\) "Olympia \(1938 film\)")_ (1938)

Preceded by[Franz Pfeffer von Salomon](/wiki/Franz_Pfeffer_von_Salomon "Franz Pfeffer von Salomon") | **[Supreme SA Leader](/wiki/Supreme_SA_Leader "Supreme SA Leader") **  
1930–1945  | **Position abolished**  
**Position established** | **[Supreme Leader of the SS](/wiki/Oberster_F%C3%BChrer_der_Schutzstaffel "Oberster Führer der Schutzstaffel") **  
1934–1945   
Military offices   
Preceded by[Paul von Hindenburg](/wiki/Paul_von_Hindenburg "Paul von Hindenburg") _**as Supreme Commander of the[Reichswehr](/wiki/Reichswehr "Reichswehr") **_ | **Supreme Commander of the _[Wehrmacht](/wiki/Wehrmacht "Wehrmacht")_ **

Preceded by[Walther von Brauchitsch](/wiki/Walther_von_Brauchitsch "Walther von Brauchitsch") | **[Commander-in-Chief of the German Army](/wiki/Oberkommando_des_Heeres#Commander-in-Chief_of_the_Army "Oberkommando des Heeres") **
